│ as np from pathlib import Path import pystan                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ def main(): # Load data train_X = pd.read_csv("/app/train_X.csv") train_y =  │
│ pd.read_csv("/app/train_y.csv") test_X = pd.read_csv("/app/test_X.csv") meta │
│ = json.load(open("/app/meta_public.json", "r"))                              │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Hyperparameters from meta                                                 │
│  P = int(meta.get(&quot;P&quot;, 3))                                         │
│  jitter = float(meta.get(&quot;jitter&quot;, 1e-6))                          │
│  # seed is fixed to 1 for reproducibility with Stan sampling                 │
│  seed = 1                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  # Data preparation                                                          │
│  X = train_X.values  # N x D                                                 │
│  y = train_y.values.squeeze()                                                │
│  N, D = X.shape                                                              │
│  X_new = test_X.values                                                       │
│  N_new = X_new.shape[0]                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  # Design matrices H = [1, x1, x2]                                           │
│  if D &lt; 2:                                                                │
│      raise ValueError(&quot;Expected at least two feature columns in         │
│  train_X.csv&quot;)                                                          │
│  H = np.column_stack((np.ones(N), X[:, 0:2]))  # N x 3                       │
│  H_new = np.column_stack((np.ones(N_new), X_new[:, 0:2]))  # N_new x 3       │
│                                                                              │
│  # Stan data dictionary (keys must match Stan data block)                    │
│  data = {                                                                    │
│      &#x27;N&#x27;: int(N),                                                  │
│      &#x27;D&#x27;: int(D),                                                  │
│      &#x27;X&#x27;: X,                                                       │
│      &#x27;P&#x27;: int(P),                                                  │
│      &#x27;H&#x27;: H,                                                       │
│      &#x27;y&#x27;: y,                                                       │
│      &#x27;jitter&#x27;: float(jitter),                                      │
│      &#x27;N_new&#x27;: int(N_new),                                          │
│      &#x27;X_new&#x27;: X_new,                                               │
│      &#x27;H_new&#x27;: H_new                                                │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Stan model definition                                                     │
│  stan_code = &quot;&quot;&quot;                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ functions { matrix cov_ard_sqexp(matrix X, vector rho, real alpha, real      │
│ jitter) { int N = rows(X); int D = cols(X); matrix[N, N] K; for (i in 1:N) { │
│ for (j in 1:N) { if (i == j) { K[i, j] = alpha * alpha + jitter; } else {    │
│ real s = 0; for (d in 1:D) s += square((X[i, d] - X[j, d]) / rho[d]); K[i,   │
│ j] = alpha * alpha * exp(-0.5 * s); } } } return K; } matrix                 │
│ cross_cov_ard(matrix X, matrix X_new, vector rho, real alpha) { int N =      │
│ rows(X); int M = rows(X_new); int D = cols(X); matrix[N, M] K; for (i in     │
│ 1:N) { for (j in 1:M) { real s = 0; for (d in 1:D) s += square((X[i, d] -    │
│ X_new[j, d]) / rho[d]); K[i, j] = alpha * alpha * exp(-0.5 * s); } } return  │
│ K; } } data { int N; int D; matrix[N, D] X; int P; matrix[N, P] H; vector[N] │
│ y; real jitter; int N_new; matrix[N_new, D] X_new; matrix[N_new, P] H_new; } │
│ parameters { vector[P] beta; real alpha; vector[D] rho; real<lower=0> sigma; │
│ } transformed parameters { vector[N] mu; matrix[N, N] K; matrix[N, N] L_K;   │
│ mu = H * beta; K = cov_ard_sqexp(X, rho, alpha, jitter) +                    │
│ diag_matrix(rep_vector(square(sigma), N)); L_K = cholesky_decompose(K); }    │
│ model { beta ~ normal(0, 1); alpha ~ normal(0, 1.5); rho ~ lognormal(0, 1);  │
│ sigma ~ normal(0, 0.5); y ~ multi_normal_cholesky(mu, L_K); } """            │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Compile and sample                                                        │
│  model = pystan.StanModel(model_code=stan_code, verbose=False)               │
│                                                                              │
│  # Sampling configuration copied from the R script: 4 chains, 2000 iters,    │
│  warmup 1000, thin 2                                                         │
│  fit = model.sampling(                                                       │
│      data=data,                                                              │
│      iter=2000,                                                              │
│      chains=4,                                                               │
│      warmup=1000,                                                            │
│      thin=2,                                                                 │
│      seed=seed,                                                              │
│      refresh=200,                                                            │
│      control={&#x27;adapt_delta&#x27;: 0.93, &#x27;max_treedepth&#x27;: 14}  │
│  )                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Extract posterior means for the required parameters                       │
│  samples = fit.extract(permuted=True)                                        │
│  alpha_post = float(np.mean(samples[&#x27;alpha&#x27;]))                     │
│  sigma_post = float(np.mean(samples[&#x27;sigma&#x27;]))                     │
│  rho_post = np.asarray(samples[&#x27;rho&#x27;]).mean(axis=0)                │
│  beta_post = np.asarray(samples[&#x27;beta&#x27;]).mean(axis=0)              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Save outputs                                                              │
│  Path(&#x27;/app&#x27;).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)                   │
│  df_alpha = pd.DataFrame({&#x27;alpha&#x27;: [alpha_post]})                  │
│  df_alpha.to_csv(&#x27;/app/alpha_est.csv&#x27;, index=False, header=False)  │
│  df_sigma = pd.DataFrame({&#x27;sigma&#x27;: [sigma_post]})                  │
│  df_sigma.to_csv(&#x27;/app/sigma_est.csv&#x27;, index=False, header=False)  │

│                                                                              │
│  # Build tools needed for compiling PyStan                                   │
│  RUN apt-get update &amp;&amp; \                                             │
│      apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \                            │
│          build-essential \                                                   │
│          gcc \                                                               │

│  RUN pip install --no-cache-dir pystan==3.10.0 pandas numpy                  │
│                                                                              │
│  # Prepare application directory inside the image                            │
│  WORKDIR /opt/pystan_app                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  # Copy the PyStan analysis script into the image                            │
│  COPY app/pystan_analysis.py /opt/pystan_app/pystan_analysis.py              │
│                                                                              │
│  # Run the analysis script by default                                        │
│  ENTRYPOINT [&quot;python3&quot;,                                            │
│  &quot;/opt/pystan_app/pystan_analysis.py&quot;]                             │

│                                                                              │
│  #!/usr/bin/env bash                                                         │

│                                                                              │
│  IMAGE=${IMAGE:-gp_pystan}                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  # Build the Docker image                                                    │
│  docker build -t &quot;$IMAGE&quot; -f docker/pystan/Dockerfile .            │
│                                                                              │
│  # Run the container with /app mounted from the host                         │
│  docker run --rm -v &quot;$(pwd)/app:/app&quot; &quot;$IMAGE&quot;           │

│  4 How to use (commands)                                                     │
│                                                                              │

│     • Use a try/except around this block so failures to print do not affect  │

│     • Compute means with numpy, handling potential single-dimension edge     │
│       cases                                                                  │
│     • Cast to float and format with reasonable precision (e.g., 6 decimals)  │
│  • Non-breaking behavior                                                     │

│ Notes:                                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • This patch assumes the PyStan 3 sampling result is stored in a variable   │
│    named fit and that you can call fit.extract(permuted=True) to obtain a    │
│    dictionary with keys 'alpha', 'sigma', 'rho', and 'beta'.                 │
│  • If your code uses a different variable name for the fit object, replace   │
│    fit with that variable name in the patch.                                 │
│  • The stdout printing is guarded by a try/except and controlled via the     │
│    environment variable PRINT_SUMMARY (default "1"). If PRINT_SUMMARY is     │
│    "0", nothing is printed.                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Run guidance (with stdout validation enabled by default)                     │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Build the Docker image (if you’re using the containerized workflow)       │
│                                                                              │
│  • docker build -t gp_pystan -f docker/pystan/Dockerfile .                   │
│                                                                              │
│  2 Run the container with data mounted                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • docker run --rm -e PRINT_SUMMARY=1 -v "$(pwd)/app:/app" gp_pystan         │
│                                                                              │
│  3 Validate outputs                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Inside host or container, verify files exist and are numeric without      │
│    headers:                                                                  │
│     • ls -l app                                                              │
│     • tail -n +1 app/alpha_est.csv                                           │
│     • tail -n +1 app/sigma_est.csv                                           │
│     • tail -n +1 app/rho_est.csv                                             │
│     • tail -n +1 app/beta_est.csv                                            │
│  • To view the optional stdout summary (printed by the script):              │
│     • Observe lines like:                                                    │
│        • ALPHA_MEAN: <value>                                                 │
│        • SIGMA_MEAN: <value>                                                 │
│        • RHO_MEAN: v1,v2,v3                                                  │
│        • BETA_MEAN: b1,b2,b3                                                 │
│  • If you want to silence stdout, run with PRINT_SUMMARY=0:                  │
│     • docker run --rm -e PRINT_SUMMARY=0 -v "$(pwd)/app:/app" gp_pystan      │
│                                                                              │
│ Notes                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • The four CSV outputs must remain unchanged in format (numeric values      │
│    only, no headers).                                                        │
│  • stdout printing is additive and should not affect the CSV outputs.        │
│  • If you run into a mismatch (e.g., the fit object variable name differs),  │
│    share the exact error and I’ll adjust the patch accordingly.              │
